{max} = Math\\n\\nexport class GridAlignmentLayout extends Layoutable {\\n  constructor(readonly children: Container<LayoutDOMView>) {\\n    super()\\n  }\\n\\n  /**\\n   * Raise every child's borders to the largest in its row and column, so that\\n   * aligning the frames cannot take space a child needs for its own borders.\\n   */\\n  align_borders(): void {\\n    const {children} = this\\n return\\n      }\\n      // A child's measurement must not carry the previous pass's borders, or\\n      // they could never shrink again.\\n      layout.align_border = {left: 0, top: 0, right: 0, }\\n\\n    this.set_geometry(outer, inner)\\n  }\\n\\n  override _set_geometry(outer: BBox, inner: BBox): void {\\n    super._set_geometry(outer, inner)\\n\\n    const items = this.children.map((_, }\\n    })\\n\\n    items.foreach(({r0, c0, r1, c1}, {layout, size_hint, bbox}) => {\\n      const outer_bbox = bbox\\n      const inner_bbox = size_hint.inner == null ? undefined : (() => {\\n {"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-5-5","id":"msg_vrtx_011ChEmYJdVtxeyWg5KCUE23","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"text","text":"I fixed four bugs in how charts are laid out when placed together. One caveat first: `recording.mp4` wasn't there (`/app/.harbor_assets/` is empty), so I couldn't watch the clip. I found the bugs by checking the layout code against the expected behavior, and I haven't checked the result visually in a browser.\n\n**What I fixed**\n\n1. **Top border ignored when lining up charts** (`src/lib/core/layout/border.ts:52`). When a chart is measured, the extra border a row asks for is applied on the left, right and bottom, but not the top. The space the chart reserves (from its CSS) uses the larger value, but the measurement used the smaller one. So in a row where one chart has a title or toolbar and its neighbour doesn't, the neighbour's frame (the area the points are drawn in) got squeezed or pushed out of line. Its labels could overlap, and its size didn't match its neighbours'.\n2. **Left and right borders taken from the wrong column** (`src/lib/models/layouts/alignments.ts:50-51`). A chart spanning several columns took its left border from its last column and its right border from its first. In a grid with spanning charts, frames didn't line up and charts could spill past their column.\n3. **Side-by-side and stacked charts never shared borders** (`src/lib/models/layouts/flex_box.ts`). Rows and columns set up the alignment layout but never ran the step that gives every chart in a row or column the same borders; only grids did. A frame with a fixed `frame_width`/`frame_height` could lose space to its neighbours' borders, and charts ended up with different heights in a row or widths in a column. Rows and columns now run that step the same way grids do.\n4. **Growing axis labels didn't trigger a new layout** (`src/lib/core/layout/side_panel.ts:247-249`). The check only re-ran layout when an axis got smaller. If tick labels got longer after a zoom, pan or data change, they were cut off. It now re-runs on any size change, so labels stay whole as the chart changes.\n\n**Testing**\n- The build succeeds and all 2431 unit tests pass (5 skipped).\n- The integration tests compare against baseline images, which this repo doesn't include, so all 1373 fail with \"missing baseline\" and can't confirm or rule out anything. I deleted the baseline files that run generated, so the only changes in the working tree are the four source files.\n\nTo confirm the fix, it's worth comparing the clip's scenario before and after."}],"container":null,"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":200,"cache_read_input_tokens":70677,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":200,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":6,"speed":"standard"},"input_transformations":[],"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"b48bf77c-6df9-4667-9154-03e83942507d","uuid":"3e17451e-fa38-4849-8e59-078b04f49c59","timestamp":"2026-11-20T19:56:17.450Z"}

So in a row where one chart has a title or toolbar and its neighbour doesn't, the neighbour's frame (the area the points are drawn in) got squeezed or pushed out of line. Its labels could overlap, and its size didn't match its neighbours'.\n2. **Left and right borders taken from the wrong column** (`src/lib/models/layouts/alignments.ts:50-51`). A chart spanning several columns took its left border from its last column and its right border from its first. In a grid with spanning charts, frames didn't line up and charts could spill past their column.\n3. **Side-by-side and stacked charts never shared borders** (`src/lib/models/layouts/flex_box.ts`). Rows and columns set up the alignment layout but never ran the step that gives every chart in a row or column the same borders; only grids did. A frame with a fixed `frame_width`/`frame_height` could lose space to its neighbours' borders, and charts ended up with different heights in a row or widths in a column. Rows and columns now run that step the same way grids do.\n4. **Growing axis labels didn't trigger a new layout** (`src/lib/core/layout/side_panel.ts:247-249`). The check only re-ran layout when an axis got smaller. If tick labels got longer after a zoom, pan or data change, they were cut off. It now re-runs on any size change, so labels stay whole as the chart changes.\n\n**Testing**\n- The build succeeds and all 2431 unit tests pass (5 skipped).\n- The integration tests compare against baseline images, which this repo doesn't include, so all 1373 fail with \"missing baseline\" and can't confirm or rule out anything.
